ParlaMint-AT_2002-05-22-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00103_d7e20785	Mr President. Mr Finanzminister! High house! I think it has already been discussed enough this evening about the mysterious links and dependencies between the tax-law reform law and the flexibilization clause. I would therefore like to devote myself to another issue from the agenda items that we are just discussing. As we all know, the terrible events of the 11. September last year several effects on the most diverse areas and unfortunately still have them. The present application for the Federal Financial Act 2002, 1130 of the Supplements, deals with the specific impact on insurance protection for air carriers and airports. Let's remember: Right after the attacks in New York, insurance companies could and did not want to assume liability for certain Austrian airlines and Austrian airports, as the risk of damage due to terror and war events was not insured under reasonable conditions. The Austrian state, like all European states, had to assume the responsibility for such risks at the earliest opportunity, otherwise the arduous aeronautics industry would have become insurmountable problems. Without adequate insurance coverage, the aircraft would not be allowed to take off or land, which would have left the entire air traffic to a certain extent. As a result, the National Council authorised the Minister of Finance for Liability, under certain conditions relating to amount and time-limitation and in accordance with relevant decisions at EU level, to be extended several times by one month each, last until 31. ==References== According to the then assessment, it was not assumed that a further extension over the 31st century would take place. It would also be necessary in May. The news of the last days and weeks, however, has taught us another person. Further threats of terror, apparently even against Austria, are overshadowing the reports of new ones. [[Abg. Dr. Lichtenberger: Whenever the deadline expires, it is so!]] In view of these developments, it is expected that the existing regulation at EU level will be above the 31st level. In May, it was extended. In order to avoid competitive disadvantages for Austrian aviation and Austrian airports, Austria will, of course, have to join these measures. [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] However, in order to avoid temporary bottlenecks in the future – the empowerment was considered only for one month each – and as a purely precautionary measure, the Minister of Finance is now to be authorised to assume the assumed liabilities up to a maximum of 31. If necessary, extend it for a period of one month. [[Abg. Dr. Lichtenberger: That was the prophecy when we discussed it! Everyone has sworn stone and leg, it won't be like that!]] This procedure should be welcomed in principle for reasons of time and administration. But it must be clear, ladies and gentlemen, that this must not be a permanent state. [[Abg. Dr. Lichtenberger: Yes, exactly!]] Insurers and, above all, reinsurers whose basic business is to cover the risks of others, and who, of course, also pay corresponding premiums, cannot simply shift their responsibility to the state, if the risk actually threatens, and take over the responsibility again and register the premiums when the thunderstorm clouds are withdrawn. If this were to be a permanent state, the State could assume this liability and the corresponding premiums equally, i.e. also in quiet times, which would not be in the interest of the State or the insurance industry. [[Demonstratel accompaniment of the abg. Dr. Lichtenberger]] [[Abg. Dr. Lichtenberger: The insurance industry already, Mrs. Colleague! They'd be very interested in it!]] We should therefore urgently act on the EU to ensure that this exceptional state does not become a permanent state.

From our point of view, this independent financial senate is an important sub-project of a comprehensive, fundamental reform of the Austrian financial administration, a reform of this financial management towards an even greater service orientation than is already the case today, to even greater efficiency, to even greater flexibility, to even greater cost-effectiveness from the taxpayer's point of view, to an organisation whose principles are, from our point of view, citizens' orientation, tax justice and fiscal equality in an enhanced form, economy, personal responsibility and performance orientation. These are principles, ladies and gentlemen, which should apply to financial management in the same way as to the independent financial Senate in the narrower sense. The independent financial Senate will strengthen citizens' rights, which is particularly important in our view, because we will have independent and independent Senate members there because we will achieve a reduction in the duration of completion because, because we have only one authority, there will be a uniform legal opinion because there will be performance transparency and, through seven locations provided for in this law on decision-making, decentralized structures. From our point of view, therefore, this independent financial Senate is a very important reform. It is 290 people and 9 000 people who are affected by it, and of course it has a pillar of financial reform also having an impact on the other elements of our financial organisation. It is clear, for example, that the financial directorates need to experience a significant change in their own right, as well as the financial offices. That means, of course, the independent financial Senate affects the entire ongoing reform process. The financial management will no longer be the second instance. It will hand over a 20 per cent share of its staff to this independent financial Senate, which is now active in the field of legal means. We will try to achieve a re-definition of the financial country directives in the sense of an enhanced service and advisory function for the financial offices. Financial country directors will be more active in the areas of quality assurance, internal revision and the fight against fraud. We will also achieve that we can reduce bureaucracy and administration and thus achieve substantial cost savings for taxpayers. [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] Mrs Hagenhofer, you have just come to terms with such intense discussion. I therefore mention this and have put this financial reform part at the beginning of my remarks, because you have said you do not know what the Minister of Finance wants and what he can do. [[Abg. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen! How can we actually address the concerns and problems of the population there? That is why I can tell you: The main focus of our reform is to strengthen the financial offices! [[Abg. Hagenhofer: Then you could have written it in, Minister of State!]] This is about the independent financial Senate and not about the financial offices. But both Kurt Heindl and you have raised the question: What does the Minister of Finance want in the financial reform? – That is why I wanted to present here briefly and explain what we want to do in the broader sense of the financial reform. I'm telling you, too. Today we have 80 financial offices; these financial offices will allow each individual to exist organisationally, but in formal competence we will create 43 economic areas in Austria. We are currently running a pilot experiment in two economic areas: on the one hand in the Mühlviertel with original driving, Freistadt and Rohrbach and on the other hand in Burgenland with Eisenstadt, Oberwart and Bruck. There are three financial offices affected each with a total of 260 employees, and there we are practically carrying out a pilot project of the financial office of the future: What will it look like in the future? What perspectives and visions we have, we are now trying to present in this pilot experiment, with the following objectives: We make the hierarchies flatter, we take hierarchies out of the organization. We are restructuring from working part-time to working opportunities in greater team orientation of our employees. We build information centers for citizens' contacts so that the concept of service to the population is more prominent. We strengthen the risk analysis, we strengthen the diversity of audits and we give management skills – as I have already said – to the financial offices and to the economic areas. We are in a good discussion process with staff management and employees. This is not a reform – I can assure you – in which we, from the Ministry of Finance, would decree from the top to the bottom, but this is done within the framework of a transparent process. That is, we have tried to shape this reform process from below, to involve the staff representation, to involve the employees and to achieve that they identify with this process. Just as a company would be reformed, so we also try to reform our entire financial organisation and thus allow employees to participate in this development, convince them, achieve identification, and thus receive a support from employees in this reform project. If we see that these two pilot projects work from the point of view of the population and from the point of view of the region, we will carry out a comprehensive implementation of this project throughout Austria in 2003. On the one hand, we will achieve a significant impact on the reform with this independent financial Senate, and at the same time we will move forward several other reform projects: We will move forward with the electronic tax declaration, we will push forward the reform of the VAT and we will move forward with the merger of the examination of pay-related taxes. We will also, we have discussed this here, carry out greater control of illegal foreign employment, and we will also carry out the task of a customs reform in this whole of the Connex. Finally, I should like to focus briefly on reform in the narrower sense. What do these independent financial Senates bring us? We know that today the two-in-a-kind judicial procedure for the tax law to be implemented by the federal government and the financial criminal law is being adopted by the seven financial country directors and that the decision is taken in Senates and individual officials bound by instructions. With this reform, we want to create nothing but an independent financial Senate, an independent administrative authority for the whole of the two-seconds legal proceedings, i.e. an independent financial Senate with jurisdiction for appeals in matters of taxation, for complaints in customs matters and for appeals in financial proceedings. We want to strengthen citizens' rights, and we want to achieve an approximation of the standards of legal protection to those of the independent Bundesasylsenate and the independent management Senate of the countries. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] Key key points and also progress in this reform compared to the status quo are that we will no longer have mixed use in specialist and legal media agendas, as is the case today. In this reform, we have provided for rules on service and remuneration which ensure the independence of employees. We achieve greater alignment of the legal protection standards with the principles of civil rights. 6 para. 1 of the Convention on Human Rights. We fulfil the criteria of a court within the meaning of the EC Treaty or the Charter of Fundamental Rights of the European Union, according to which each person has the right to have their case negotiated publicly and within a reasonable period of time by an independent, impartial court established by law. We relieve this reform – it has already been addressed – of the Administrative Court and the Constitutional Court. Of course, we take into account the principles of economy, economy and effectiveness. We do not build any restrictions on access to the public – for example, a fee obligation or a cost-refund scheme – as is the case with courts, because we have said: As open as possible, free access should be guaranteed to the public. We maintain typical legal instruments for the administrative procedure. In total, we create a flexible government organisation, flexible processes and an efficient, effective organisation of independent financial Senates. We achieve organisationally the integration of the entire two-in-a-kind legal system of financial and customs administration. I would now like to address one point, namely Article VII Z 1, which you mentioned, and the separate vote you have referred to. I say quite frankly to you that on your side there must obviously be some mistake in the interpretation of this Article VII Z 1, because it does not give the Minister of Finance the authority to do and to allow it to do organizationally in Austria, but it concerns exactly a financial office throughout Austria, namely the Financial Office for Fees in Vienna. For no other financial office, this Article VII Z 1 shall apply! We are here in agreement with the Vienna organization – with President Frey of the Finanzlandesdirektion Wien, Niederösterreich and Burgenland – to bring together the evaluation bodies, which are now divided into several financial offices in Vienna and cannot carry out the evaluation efficiently, because there is this fragmentation in a financial office. This financial office is the financial office for fees. Everything else is not affected. I just wanted to clarify this so that you don't have the impression that this is an authorisation for the Minister of Finance, on the basis of which he can do and leave whatever he wants. This is not the case, but it applies to a financial office, the financial office for fees, and it serves to bring together the assessment bodies efficiently, exclusively in Vienna. Nothing else is the intention of this section. Ladies and gentlemen! To sum up, this is a truly successful reform from my point of view. We have taken up a long-standing need for action. I would like to thank all the political groups for the possibility of reaching consensus on a reform which is not a public issue, but which has direct and practical impact on the population, on every individual citizen in our country, as in the case of financial market supervision, even with this independent financial Senate. As far as the flexibilisation clause is concerned, Mr Edlinger has precisely celebrated this issue in his presentation. I can only say: I would like to thank the fact that the continuation of this "flex clause" will be possible, and I have always said – you have mentioned it – that I am in favour of this clause.
